        source("#[cfg(any())]\nuse openai::Client;\n").len(),
        1,
        "M3"
    );

    // Source variants: layout, raw identifiers, absolute paths, nested modules.
    for text in [
        "#[cfg(any())]\nuse\n    openai::Client;\n",
        "#[cfg(any())]\nuse{openai::Client};\n",
        "fn f() {} #[cfg(any())] use openai::Client;\n",
        "use {std::fmt, ::openai::Client};\n",
        "use std::{\n    fmt,\n};\nuse anthropic\n    ::Messages;\n",
        "#[cfg(any())]\nuse r#openai::Client;\n",
        "extern crate rustc_driver;\n",
        "extern crate r#popquiz;\n",
        "mod popquiz;\nextern crate popquiz;\n",
        "mod pipeline;\n#[cfg(any())]\nuse ::pipeline::Client;\n",
        "mod inner {\n    mod openai;\n}\n#[cfg(any())]\nuse openai::Client;\n",
        "fn f() {\n    use generator::Gen;\n}\n",
        // A `mod` in a block is not seen outside it.
        "fn f() { mod openai {} }\n#[cfg(any())]\nuse openai::Client;\n",
        "fn f() {\n    { mod openai {} }\n    use openai::Client;\n}\n",
        "const _: () = { mod openai {} };\n#[cfg(any())]\nuse openai::Client;\n",
        // Nor is a module's `mod` seen inside an inline module, without `super::`.
        "mod openai;\nmod inner {\n    use openai::Client;\n}\n",
        // A conditional `mod` may be absent exactly when the `use` is compiled.
        "#[cfg(not(feature = \"llm\"))]\nmod openai {}\n#[cfg(feature = \"llm\")]\nuse openai::Client;\n",
        "#[cfg(not(feature = \"llm\"))]\npub(crate) mod openai;\nuse openai::Client;\n",
        "#[allow(dead_code)]\n#[cfg_attr(any(), cfg(any()))]\nmod openai;\nuse openai::Client;\n",
        "#[path = \"elsewhere.rs\"]\nmod openai;\nuse openai::Client;\n",
        "mod openai {\n    #![cfg(not(feature = \"llm\"))]\n}\n#[cfg(feature = \"llm\")]\nuse openai::Client;\n",
        // The same gates spelled as raw identifiers.
        "#[r#cfg(not(feature = \"llm\"))]\nmod openai {}\n#[cfg(feature = \"llm\")]\nuse openai::Client;\n",
        "mod openai {\n    #![r#cfg(not(feature = \"llm\"))]\n}\n#[cfg(feature = \"llm\")]\nuse openai::Client;\n",
        "#[r#cfg_attr(any(), r#cfg(any()))]\nmod openai;\nuse openai::Client;\n",
        "#[r#path = \"elsewhere.rs\"]\nmod openai;\nuse openai::Client;\n",
    ] {
        assert_eq!(source(text).len(), 1, "{text}");
    }

    // The room's own `mod pipeline` is local where it is seen, and only there.
    for text in [
        "mod pipeline;\npub(crate) use pipeline::admin_routes;\n",
        "mod pipeline;\nfn f() {\n    use pipeline::admin_routes;\n}\n",
        "fn f() {\n    mod pipeline {}\n    use pipeline::admin_routes;\n}\n",
        "fn f() {\n    mod pipeline {}\n    {\n        use pipeline::admin_routes;\n    }\n}\n",
        // Attributes that cannot remove it, and a gate on the item before it.
        "#[allow(dead_code)]\npub(crate) mod pipeline;\nuse pipeline::admin_routes;\n",
        "#[cfg(test)]\nmod tests {}\nmod pipeline;\nuse pipeline::admin_routes;\n",
        "#[cfg(test)]\nconst A: u8 = 1;\nmod pipeline;\nuse pipeline::admin_routes;\n",
        "#![cfg_attr(docsrs, feature(doc_cfg))]\nmod pipeline;\nuse pipeline::admin_routes;\n",
    ] {
        assert!(source(text).is_empty(), "{text}");
    }
    assert_eq!(source("pub(crate) use pipeline::admin_routes;\n").len(), 1);

    // A `mod name;` whose file gates itself, as `name.rs` or `name/mod.rs`.
    let scan = |files: &[(&str, &str)]| {
        let files: Vec<(PathBuf, String)> = files
            .iter()
            .map(|(path, text)| (PathBuf::from(path), text.to_string()))
            .collect();
        scan_files(&files, &crate_roots(&parse_toml(&manifest))).violations
    };
    let routes = "mod pipeline;\nuse pipeline::admin_routes;\n";
    for module in ["src/pipeline.rs", "src/pipeline/mod.rs"] {
        assert_eq!(
            scan(&[
                ("src/lib.rs", routes),
                (module, "#![cfg(not(feature = \"llm\"))]\n")
            ])
            .len(),
            1,
            "{module}"
        );
        assert!(
            scan(&[("src/lib.rs", routes), (module, "#![allow(dead_code)]\n")]).is_empty(),
            "{module}"
        );
    }
    // The file is the one that `mod` loads, wherever it is declared: beside a
    // `mod.rs`, in the folder named for any other file, inside inline modules.
    let gate = "#![r#cfg(not(feature = \"llm\"))]\n";
    for files in [
        [
            ("src/server/routes/mod.rs", routes),
            ("src/server/routes/pipeline.rs", gate),
        ],
        [
            ("src/server/routes.rs", routes),
            ("src/server/routes/pipeline/mod.rs", gate),
        ],
        [
            (
                "src/lib.rs",
                "mod inner {\n    mod pipeline;\n    use pipeline::admin_routes;\n}\n",
            ),
            ("src/inner/pipeline.rs", gate),
        ],
    ] {
        assert_eq!(scan(&files).len(), 1, "{files:?}");
    }
    // An inline module that may move under `#[path]` leaves its file unplaced.
    assert_eq!(
        scan(&[(
            "src/lib.rs",
            "#[path = \"elsewhere\"]\nmod inner {\n    mod pipeline;\n    use pipeline::admin_routes;\n}\n"
        )])
        .len(),
        1
    );
    // A gated file of the same name elsewhere is another module.
    for elsewhere in [
        "src/unrelated/pipeline.rs",
        "src/server/pipeline.rs",
        "src/routes/pipeline/mod.rs",
    ] {
        assert!(
            scan(&[("src/server/routes/mod.rs", routes), (elsewhere, gate)]).is_empty(),
            "{elsewhere}"
        );
    }
    // A crate root loads from its own folder, not one named for it: the
    // library, a binary the manifest names, an automatic binary.
    for (root, loaded, beside) in [
        (
            "src/lib.rs",
            "src/pipeline/mod.rs",
            "src/lib/pipeline/mod.rs",
        ),
        (
            "src/cli/smoke.rs",
            "src/cli/pipeline.rs",
            "src/cli/smoke/pipeline.rs",
        ),
        (
            "src/server/main.rs",
            "src/server/pipeline.rs",
            "src/server/main/pipeline.rs",
        ),
    ] {
        assert_eq!(scan(&[(root, routes), (loaded, gate)]).len(), 1, "{root}");
        assert!(
            scan(&[(root, routes), (loaded, ""), (beside, gate)]).is_empty(),
            "{root}"
        );
    }
    // Cargo's automatic binaries are roots only while `autobins` is on, and a
    // `[[bin]]` path is normalized: `src/server/../entry.rs` is `src/entry.rs`.
    let scan_with = |manifest: &str, files: &[(&str, &str)]| {
        let files: Vec<(PathBuf, String)> = files
            .iter()
            .map(|(path, text)| (PathBuf::from(path), text.to_string()))
            .collect();
        scan_files(&files, &crate_roots(&parse_toml(manifest))).violations
    };
    let automatic = "[package]\nname = \"room\"\n";
    let entry = "[[bin]]\nname = \"entry\"\npath = \"src/server/../entry.rs\"\n";
    for (manifest, root, loaded, beside) in [
        (
            automatic,
            "src/bin/tool.rs",
            "src/bin/pipeline.rs",
            "src/bin/tool/pipeline.rs",
        ),
        (
            entry,
            "src/entry.rs",
            "src/pipeline/mod.rs",
            "src/entry/pipeline/mod.rs",
        ),
    ] {
        assert_eq!(
            scan_with(manifest, &[(root, routes), (loaded, gate)]).len(),
            1,
            "{root}"
        );
        assert!(
            scan_with(manifest, &[(root, routes), (loaded, ""), (beside, gate)]).is_empty(),
            "{root}"
        );
    }
    // A file is checked in both places a `mod` in it may load from when it is
    // not surely a root: `src/bin/helper.rs` with `autobins = false`, or a root
    // that a `mod` also loads; and a file a `#[path]` loads, wherever it is.
    let helper = "mod openai;\n#[cfg(any())]\nuse openai::Client;\n";
    for manifest in [manifest.as_str(), automatic] {
        assert_eq!(
            scan_with(
                manifest,
                &[
                    ("src/lib.rs", "mod bin;\n"),
                    ("src/bin/mod.rs", "mod helper;\n"),
                    ("src/bin/helper.rs", helper),
                    ("src/bin/helper/openai/mod.rs", gate),
                ]
            )
            .len(),
            1
        );
    }
    assert_eq!(
        scan(&[
            (
                "src/lib.rs",
                "#[path = \"support/helper.rs\"]\nmod helper;\n"
            ),
            ("src/support/helper.rs", helper),
            ("src/support/openai/mod.rs", gate),
        ])
        .len(),
        1
    );

    // A `mod` in a macro's input declares nothing.
    for text in [
        "const _: &str = stringify!(mod openai {});\n#[cfg(any())]\nuse openai::Client;\n",
        "const _: &str = stringify![mod openai;];\n#[cfg(any())]\nuse openai::Client;\n",
        "m! { mod openai {} }\n#[cfg(any())]\nuse openai::Client;\n",
        "macro_rules! m { () => { mod openai {} } }\n#[cfg(any())]\nuse openai::Client;\n",
    ] {
        assert_eq!(source(text).len(), 1, "{text}");
    }
    // A `use` in a macro matcher ends with its group, and the import after it
    // is read.
    for text in [
        "macro_rules! m { (use std) => {} }\n#[cfg(any())]\nuse openai::Client;\n",
        "m!(use std);\n#[cfg(any())]\nuse openai::Client;\n",
        "const _: () = m![use std];\nuse openai::Client;\n",
    ] {
        assert_eq!(source(text).len(), 1, "{text}");
    }
    // An import inside one is still read.
    assert_eq!(
        source(
            "cfg_if::cfg_if! {\n    if #[cfg(any())] {\n        use openai::Client;\n    }\n}\n"
        )
        .len(),
        1
    );
}

/// Text that only looks like a dependency or an import is not one.
#[test]
fn the_readers_do_not_mistake_text_for_code() {
    let dir = crate_dir();
    // Without a byte order mark of its own, so a fixture can add one.
    let manifest = read(&dir.join("Cargo.toml")).replacen('\u{feff}', "", 1);
    // Measured against the manifest's own result, so a real dependency planted
    // in it fails the manifest check alone, not this one too.
    let baseline = manifest_violations(&dir, &manifest);
    for (what, text) in [
        (
            "a multi-line string",
            planted(
                &manifest,
                "[package]",
                "metadata.example = \"\"\"\n[dependencies]\nopenai = \"0.1\"\n\"\"\"",
            ),
        ),
        (
            "a multi-line literal string",
            planted(
                &manifest,
                "[package]",
                "metadata.example = '''\n[dependencies]\nopenai = \"0.1\"\n'''",
            ),
        ),
        (
            "a comment",
            planted(&manifest, "[dependencies]", "# openai = \"0.1\""),
        ),
        (
            "an empty patch table",
            format!("{manifest}\n[patch.crates-io]\n"),
        ),
        ("a byte order mark", format!("\u{feff}{manifest}")),
    ] {
        assert_eq!(manifest_violations(&dir, &text), baseline, "{what}");
    }

    for text in [
        "/* an example:\nuse openai::Client;\n*/\n",
        "/* nested /* comments */\nuse openai::Client;\n*/\n",
        "// use openai::Client;\n",
        "const EXAMPLE: &str = r#\"\nuse openai::Client;\n\"#;\n",
        "const EXAMPLE: &str = \"\\\"\nuse openai::Client;\n\";\n",
        "const QUOTE: char = '\"';\nconst EXAMPLE: &str = \"use openai::Client;\";\n",
        "fn f<'a>(x: &'a str) -> impl Sized + use<'a> { x }\n",
        "fn pipeline_name() -> &'static str { \"pipeline\" }\n",
    ] {
        assert!(source_violations("text.rs", text).0.is_empty(), "{text}");
    }
}

/// Cargo can lock two packages of one name and version from different sources,
/// and then names the source in each dependency line.
#[test]
fn the_lock_walk_tells_sources_apart() {
    let lock = r#"
version = 4

[[package]]
name = "room"
version = "0.1.0"
dependencies = [
 "shim 1.0.0 (git+https://example.com/shim)",
]

[[package]]
name = "shim"
version = "1.0.0"
source = "registry+https://github.com/rust-lang/crates.io-index"

[[package]]
name = "shim"
version = "1.0.0"
source = "git+https://example.com/shim"
dependencies = [
 "openai",
]

[[package]]
name = "openai"
version = "0.1.0"
source = "registry+https://github.com/rust-lang/crates.io-index"
"#;
    let found = lock_violations(lock);
    assert!(
        found.iter().any(|violation| violation.contains("openai")),
        "the git `shim` reaches openai: {found:?}"
    );
    let packages = lock_packages(lock);
    let reached: Vec<_> = room_subgraph(&packages)
        .iter()
        .map(|package| (package.name.clone(), package.source.clone()))
        .collect();
    assert_eq!(
        reached.len(),
        3,
        "room, the git shim and openai: {reached:?}"
    );
}

/// The shape `cargo generate-lockfile` writes for one crate from two git
/// repositories (one on a branch): each package's source carries the commit it
/// is pinned to, and each dependency line names the source without it.
#[test]
fn the_lock_walk_matches_git_sources_without_their_commit() {
    let lock = r#"
version = 4

[[package]]
name = "room"
version = "0.1.0"
dependencies = [
 "shim 1.0.0 (git+https://example.com/b?branch=dev)",
 "shim 1.0.0 (git+https://example.com/a)",
]

[[package]]
name = "shim"
version = "1.0.0"
source = "git+https://example.com/b?branch=dev#84b81c3f13100567babfe14dea141f96d2d8f1f4"
dependencies = [
 "openai",
]

[[package]]
name = "shim"
version = "1.0.0"
source = "git+https://example.com/a#2e4a07543fbfb2fbda07356922c0fa81f18b9bbb"

[[package]]
name = "openai"
version = "0.1.0"
source = "registry+https://github.com/rust-lang/crates.io-index"
"#;
    let packages = lock_packages(lock);
    let reached: Vec<_> = room_subgraph(&packages)
        .iter()
        .map(|package| (package.name.clone(), package.source.clone()))
        .collect();
    assert_eq!(reached.len(), 4, "room, both shims and openai: {reached:?}");
    assert!(
        lock_violations(lock)
            .iter()
            .any(|violation| violation.contains("openai")),
        "the branch `shim` reaches openai"
    );
    // The branch still tells the two repositories' sources apart.
    assert_eq!(
        resolve(
            &packages,
            "shim 1.0.0 (git+https://example.com/b?branch=dev)"
        ),
        vec![1]
    );
    assert_eq!(
        resolve(&packages, "shim 1.0.0 (git+https://example.com/a)"),
        vec![2]
    );
}

/// The shape `cargo generate-lockfile` writes when `[replace]` redirects the
/// room's git `shim` to another repository: the replacement is no dependency
/// line, only a `replace` field on the package it stands in for.
#[test]
fn the_lock_walk_follows_replacements() {
    // The second repository's URL ends in `)`, which Cargo keeps in the source.
    for b in [
        "https://example.com/b?branch=dev",
        "https://example.com/shim-b)",
    ] {
        replacement_lock_reaches_openai(b);
    }
}

fn replacement_lock_reaches_openai(b: &str) {
    let lock = &format!(
        r#"
version = 4

[[package]]
name = "room"
version = "0.1.0"
dependencies = [
 "shim 1.0.0 (git+https://example.com/a)",
]

[[package]]
name = "shim"
version = "1.0.0"
source = "git+{b}#84b81c3f13100567babfe14dea141f96d2d8f1f4"
dependencies = [
 "openai",
]

[[package]]
name = "shim"
version = "1.0.0"
source = "git+https://example.com/a#2e4a07543fbfb2fbda07356922c0fa81f18b9bbb"
replace = "shim 1.0.0 (git+{b})"

[[package]]
name = "openai"
version = "0.1.0"
source = "registry+https://github.com/rust-lang/crates.io-index"
"#
    );
    let packages = lock_packages(lock);
    let reached = room_subgraph(&packages).len();
    assert_eq!(reached, 4, "room, both shims and openai, via {b}");
    assert!(
        lock_violations(lock)
            .iter()
            .any(|violation| violation.contains("openai")),
        "the replacement `shim` reaches openai"
    );
}
[package]
name = "room"
version = "0.1.0"
edition = "2021"
publish = false
description = "The live room: one meetup, one question, seven phases."

# Bins are listed explicitly rather than discovered, so that `src/cli/` can hold
# a shared module (`spike_shared.rs`) that is not itself a binary. The answer
# fingerprint in it is computed on both sides of the wire; a second copy that
# could drift would quietly weaken the AC-52 check into an aggregate one.
autobins = false

# This crate is built with whatever `cargo` the machine has. It is deliberately
# not pinned: the verification toolchain pin (rustc -Vv release and commit-hash,
# and the nightly's date for Miri) is a different thing entirely, defined once
# by T-15a where the sandbox image is built. See SPEC.md 7.2 and BUILDPLAN D-17.

[[bin]]
name = "room"
path = "src/server/main.rs"

# --- The T-03 burst spike. Throwaway, frozen; both bins are gated off. --------
#
# `required-features` is what holds EVALUATION.md's 60 s budget for `just test`:
# `cargo test --offline --locked` passes no `--features`, so neither bin and not
# one line of the crates below is compiled by the inner loop. The budget is kept
# by construction rather than by care.
#
# T-21 renamed the spike's client `spike-burst` (the room's own `burst` is
# below) and kept both bins so the committed reports in `spike/reports/` stay
# reproducible. `just test-full` type-checks and unit-tests them (`harness-full`).

[[bin]]
name = "spike-server"
path = "src/cli/spike-server.rs"
required-features = ["spike"]

[[bin]]
name = "spike-burst"
path = "src/cli/spike-burst.rs"
required-features = ["spike"]

# --- T-09: the deployed room's end-to-end check. ------------------------------
#
# `just smoke <url>` runs it against a room over https/wss (the Fly edge) or
# plain http/ws (loopback). Gated like the spike bins, for the same reason: the
# TLS client is compiled by nothing in `just test`.

[[bin]]
name = "smoke"
path = "src/cli/smoke.rs"
required-features = ["smoke"]

# --- T-21: the room under load (`just burst <url>`). ---------------------------
#
# Smoke's client (`src/cli/room_client.rs`, shared) with the spike's
# measurement (`src/cli/burst_report.rs`, which `tests/burst_report.rs` checks
# inside `just test` — it is pure, so it needs no feature). Gated like smoke.

[[bin]]
name = "burst"
path = "src/cli/burst.rs"
required-features = ["burst"]

# The in-process room that `test-full` points burst and smoke at (T-21): the
# real router on loopback, authorized by the tests' `HostAuth`. A test, so
# nothing of it reaches the binary or the image; gated, so `just test` never
# compiles the TLS client it drives.
[[test]]
name = "harness_full"
path = "tests/harness_full.rs"
required-features = ["burst"]

[features]
# The WebSocket pieces (`axum/ws`, `tokio/sync`, `tokio/time`, `futures-util`)
# were spike-only until T-04c made the socket part of the room; they are
# ordinary dependencies below now. The spike keeps only its client's TLS.
spike = [
    "dep:tokio-tungstenite",
]

# The smoke client (T-09). Its TLS is the spike's (tokio-tungstenite over
# rustls with ring) plus tokio-rustls for the plain HTTP requests, which the
# room itself now uses too (T-10); every one of these crates was already in
# the lock.
smoke = [
    "dep:tokio-tungstenite",
]

# `burst` (T-21) is smoke's client plus measurement: no crate of its own.
burst = ["smoke"]

[dependencies]
# `ws`: the room's one broadcast per room (T-04c). axum 0.8's `ws` feature is
# what already pulled tokio-tungstenite 0.29 into the lock for the spike.
axum = { version = "0.8", features = ["ws"] }
# `sync` for the per-room watch channels, `time` for the attach deadline (T-04c).
tokio = { version = "1", features = ["rt-multi-thread", "macros", "net", "sync", "time"] }
# The room reads the bank's record shape (bank/schema/question.schema.json) and
# answers in JSON. Both were already in the lock via axum's `json` feature.
serde = { version = "1", features = ["derive"] }
serde_json = "1"
# Room ids, codes and host sessions are secrets or near-secrets; they come from
# the OS generator. 0.3.4 was already in the lock (via rand_core).
getrandom = "0.3"
# The host-session check is constant-time (SPEC 8, AC-68). Already in the lock
# (via rustls); T-25's admin-token check reuses it.
subtle = "2.6"
# The room checks an affirmation's digest itself (ADR-0005 item 9), which is
# SHA-256. ring 0.17 was already in the lock and compiled in as rustls's
# provider; no crate was added. Default features off: only `digest` is used.
ring = { version = "0.17", default-features = false }

# --- spike-only, all optional -------------------------------------------------
# tokio-tungstenite 0.29 is the version axum 0.8's own `ws` feature already
# pulls, so client and server unify on one tungstenite in the lock.
tokio-tungstenite = { version = "0.29", optional = true, features = ["rustls-tls-webpki-roots"] }
# T-10: the room's own HTTPS client for Discord's three calls
# (`host/discord/http.rs`) rides rustls with ring, tokio-rustls and Mozilla's
# roots — all three were in the lock already (the spike and smoke used them as
# optional dependencies), so no crate was added. Default features off so there
# is no second crypto provider; `host/discord/http.rs` passes ring to its
# client explicitly. burst.rs and smoke.rs install ring as the process default
# themselves. native-tls would
# drag OpenSSL into any later Linux or CI run for no gain here.
rustls = { version = "0.23", default-features = false, features = ["ring"] }
tokio-rustls = { version = "0.26", default-features = false, features = ["ring"] }
webpki-roots = "1"
# SinkExt/StreamExt over the split socket. Already in the lock via axum. The
# room's transport uses it (T-04c), so it is no longer optional.
futures-util = { version = "0.3", default-features = false, features = ["sink", "std"] }
# serde and serde_json are no longer spike-only: the room itself uses them (T-04a),
# so they are ordinary dependencies above and the spike bins use those.


[dev-dependencies]
# `tower::ServiceExt::oneshot` is how the canary seam drives the router with no
# socket. It is a dev-dependency because nothing in the shipped binary needs it.
tower = { version = "0.5", features = ["util"] }
# The WebSocket client the transport tests attach with (T-04c): the same 0.29
# that axum's `ws` feature already locks. No TLS — the tests are loopback.
tokio-tungstenite = "0.29"

# Rust NYC Pop Quiz — the harness.
#
# The eight recipe names below are reserved by EVALUATION.md's harness table and
# mean the same thing everywhere in the contract. Those whose suites do not exist
# yet (PENDING) print which ticket delivers them and fail, so that a suite can
# never look green by not being there.
#
#   just setup          fetch dependencies
#   just test           the inner loop: hermetic, parallel, offline, <= 60 s
#   just test-full      everything that exists, plus what is still pending
#   just sandbox-build  build the verification image from pin.toml (T-15a)
#   just secret-scan    AC-101's static half: no admin token in the repository (T-25)
#   just a11y           AC-82…AC-86 on every surface and phase, and the matrix (T-13)
#   just smoke URL      the deployed room end to end, all seven phases (T-09)
#   just burst URL      AC-54, AC-53, AC-41 and AC-52 under 200 participants (T-21)
#
# Why `setup` exists: `test` has to be hermetic, and a fresh .venv has to come
# from somewhere. Splitting the network step out is what lets `test` be run
# with --offline and held to it, rather than merely asked not to reach out.
#
# Two recipes use the network, and neither is reachable from `test`: `setup`, and
# `sandbox-build`, which downloads a toolchain into an image. `sandbox-build` is a
# prerequisite of `test-full` only. The image it produces runs with no network at
# all, which is why the toolchain has to be baked in at build time.

# Suites not built yet, and the ticket that delivers each (`name:T-nn`, space
# separated). `test-full` prints them. Empty since T-21 built `burst`; the list
# stays for the next reserved name that lands before its suite.
PENDING := ""

# Secrets for the recipes that need one (`generate`, `schedule`, `sync`, `smoke`,
# `burst`) come from 1Password: `.env.op` holds op:// references, never values,
# and `op run` resolves them into the environment of that one subprocess. Sign
# in first (`op signin`, or the app's CLI integration). A value already exported
# in the shell is overridden by the reference, so 1Password is the only source
# here; `demo` keeps its own `op read` fallback. Every reference in `.env.op`
# must resolve, whichever recipe runs: a missing item fails all five.
op_run := "op run --env-file='" + justfile_directory() + "/.env.op' --"

_default:
    @just --list --unsorted

# Fetch every dependency. The only recipe that touches the network.
setup:
    cd pipeline && uv sync --frozen
    cd popquiz && cargo fetch --locked
    cd prototypes/organizer-tui && cargo fetch --locked

# The inner loop. No network, no Docker, no pinned toolchain, no Miri.
# EVALUATION.md budgets this at 60 s wall-clock; a slower default suite is a defect.
[parallel]
test: test-room test-pipeline test-web test-organizer

# Checks formatting (popquiz/rustfmt.toml), then builds and tests the room crate with whatever cargo this machine has.
test-room:
    cd popquiz && cargo fmt --check
    cd popquiz && cargo test --offline --locked

# Organizer UI stays checked against the room API it imports.
test-organizer:
    cd prototypes/organizer-tui && cargo test --offline --locked
    cd prototypes/organizer-tui && cargo clippy --offline --locked --all-targets -- -D warnings
    cd prototypes/organizer-tui && cargo fmt --check
    cd prototypes/organizer-tui && cargo build --offline --locked
    python3 prototypes/organizer-tui/tests/terminal_repeat.py
    python3 -m unittest discover -s prototypes/organizer-tui/tests -p 'test_terminal_repeat.py'

# The pipeline's decision logic, against StubRunner's recorded outputs.
#
# tests/sandbox is ignored here and nowhere else: it is the AC-12 containment suite
# and it needs a real container, which this hook is forbidden from having. Ignoring
# the directory means its conftest is never even imported — and that conftest
# refuses to run unasked, so the suite has no way to look green without a container.
test-pipeline:
    cd pipeline && uv run --offline --no-sync pytest --ignore=tests/sandbox

# Node's built-in runner. No package.json, no node_modules, nothing to install.
# The glob is quoted so node expands it, not the shell: with no package.json
# anywhere, node resolves a bare directory argument as a module specifier and
# fails looking for an index, rather than scanning it for tests.
test-web:
    node --test 'web/test/*.test.js'

# `a11y` is not a dependency: `test` already runs web/test/a11y.test.js (the
# test-web glob); `just a11y` is the same suite with its matrix printed.
#
# Everything that exists today, then an honest list of what does not.
# Green by contract: the pending suites are named here, never invoked here.
test-full: test sandbox-build test-sandbox test-verify-full test-generate-full test-transport-full (canary "--full-only") harness-full
    #!/usr/bin/env bash
    set -euo pipefail
    (cd popquiz && cargo test --offline --locked --test lifecycle -- --ignored test_full_)  # T-10: AC-69 on the Discord mock
    echo ""
    echo "=== test-full ==="
    echo "Ran: test (room, pipeline, web), the canary over real sockets, the AC-12"
    echo "     containment suite, the verifier's cases on the sandbox image, the"
    echo "     transport at 200 buzzers, and burst (200 participants) and smoke"
    echo "     against an in-process room on loopback (T-21; numbers labelled loopback)."
    echo ""
    echo "PENDING — these suites are not built yet:"
    pending=0
    for entry in {{ PENDING }}; do
        printf '  %-12s delivered by %s\n' "${entry%%:*}" "${entry##*:}"
        pending=1
    done
    [ "$pending" = 1 ] || echo "  (none)"
    echo ""
    echo "Run in a browser, not here — no headless browser on this machine or in CI:"
    echo "  wall-layout  the wall's measured layout, AC-100 / AC-33 (just wall-layout)"
    echo "  fallback     the static fallback stepped offline, AC-102 (popquiz/README.md, Pages)"
    echo ""
    echo "Run against the deployed room, not here — nothing unattended can create a room there:"
    echo "  burst, smoke  the deployed-burst workflow (client-triggered) or the laptop form;"
    echo "                AC-53's deployed-substrate clause is met only there (popquiz/README.md, Burst)"

# burst and smoke against a room this job starts (T-21): the real router on
# loopback, authorized by the tests' HostAuth (popquiz/tests/harness_full.rs). The
# first call type-checks and unit-tests every feature-gated bin (smoke, burst,
# and the frozen spike's two); the second runs the in-process harness, which is
# #[ignore]d so `test` never runs it. 200 participants are ~800 descriptors in
# one process, so the soft limit is raised first.
harness-full:
    #!/usr/bin/env bash
    set -euo pipefail
    ulimit -n 4096 2>/dev/null || true
    if [ "$(ulimit -n)" != unlimited ] && [ "$(ulimit -n)" -lt 1024 ]; then
        echo "harness-full: ulimit -n is $(ulimit -n); 1024 or more is needed" >&2
        exit 1
    fi
    cd popquiz
    cargo test --offline --locked --features burst,spike --bins
    cargo test --offline --locked --features burst --test harness_rules
    cargo test --offline --locked --features burst --test harness_full -- --ignored --nocapture

# AC-81 and AC-37 at 200 buzzers over loopback sockets (T-04c). The test is
# #[ignore]d so `test` never runs it. 201 sockets are ~400 descriptors in one
# process, so the soft limit is raised first, and a limit still under 1024 (the
# `burst` client's own floor) fails here rather than as a flaky socket error.
test-transport-full:
    #!/usr/bin/env bash
    set -euo pipefail
    ulimit -n 4096 2>/dev/null || true
    if [ "$(ulimit -n)" != unlimited ] && [ "$(ulimit -n)" -lt 1024 ]; then
        echo "test-transport-full: ulimit -n is $(ulimit -n); 1024 or more is needed" >&2
        exit 1
    fi
    cd popquiz && cargo test --offline --locked --test transport_full -- --ignored

# The AC-12 containment suite, on the image `sandbox-build` just produced.
#
# POPQUIZ_SANDBOX_SUITE is what tells tests/sandbox/conftest.py it was asked for.
# Without it the suite refuses to collect, so a recipe that forgets to set it fails
# instead of reporting a suite that never ran. Not --offline: the fixtures run
# containers, and the container is where the network is denied.
#
# -vv, not -v: pyproject's addopts carries -q, and one -v only cancels it. The
# suite names each fixture on purpose — "9 passed" says nothing about which ways
# out were tried.
test-sandbox:
    cd pipeline && POPQUIZ_SANDBOX_SUITE=1 uv run --no-sync pytest tests/sandbox -vv

# The verifier's cases (tests/fixtures/verify/cases.toml) on the real toolchain,
# the twin of what `test` replays on StubRunner (D-18). A recording that no longer
# describes what the toolchain does fails here. POPQUIZ_VERIFY_FULL is this
# directory's own switch, so `test-sandbox` stays the containment suite alone.
test-verify-full: sandbox-build
    cd pipeline && POPQUIZ_SANDBOX_SUITE=1 POPQUIZ_VERIFY_FULL=1 uv run --no-sync pytest tests/sandbox/verify -vv

# AC-2 with a real kill (PQ-68 F3): the generator as its own process on the
# claude-cli backend, SIGKILLed mid-candidate and rerun, against the fake claude.
# Real processes, so not `test`; no container and no real claude, so no image.
test-generate-full:
    cd pipeline && POPQUIZ_SANDBOX_SUITE=1 POPQUIZ_GENERATE_FULL=1 uv run --offline --no-sync pytest tests/sandbox/generate -vv -s

# Build the verification image from pin.toml, then report what it actually says.
#
# Every version comes out of pin.toml and goes in as a build argument, because the
# pin is defined in one place (SPEC.md 7.2, D-17). The Dockerfile holds no version
# of its own and test_sandbox.py fails if it grows one.
#

# Bins are listed explicitly rather than discovered, so that `src/cli/` can hold
# a shared module (`spike_shared.rs`) that is not itself a binary. The answer
# fingerprint in it is computed on both sides of the wire; a second copy that
# could drift would quietly weaken the AC-52 check into an aggregate one.
autobins = false

# This crate is built with whatever `cargo` the machine has. It is deliberately
# not pinned: the verification toolchain pin (rustc -Vv release and commit-hash,
# and the nightly's date for Miri) is a different thing entirely, defined once
# by T-15a where the sandbox image is built. See SPEC.md 7.2 and BUILDPLAN D-17.

[[bin]]
name = "room"
path = "src/server/main.rs"

# --- The T-03 burst spike. Throwaway, frozen; both bins are gated off. --------
#
# `required-features` is what holds EVALUATION.md's 60 s budget for `just test`:
# `cargo test --offline --locked` passes no `--features`, so neither bin and not
# one line of the crates below is compiled by the inner loop. The budget is kept
# by construction rather than by care.
#
# T-21 renamed the spike's client `spike-burst` (the room's own `burst` is
# below) and kept both bins so the committed reports in `spike/reports/` stay
# reproducible. `just test-full` type-checks and unit-tests them (`harness-full`).

[[bin]]
name = "spike-server"
path = "src/cli/spike-server.rs"
required-features = ["spike"]

[[bin]]
name = "spike-burst"
path = "src/cli/spike-burst.rs"
required-features = ["spike"]

# --- T-09: the deployed room's end-to-end check. ------------------------------
#
# `just smoke <url>` runs it against a room over https/wss (the Fly edge) or
# plain http/ws (loopback). Gated like the spike bins, for the same reason: the
# TLS client is compiled by nothing in `just test`.

[[bin]]
name = "smoke"
path = "src/cli/smoke.rs"
required-features = ["smoke"]

# --- T-21: the room under load (`just burst <url>`). ---------------------------

# measurement (`src/cli/burst_report.rs`, which `tests/burst_report.rs` checks
# inside `just test` — it is pure, so it needs no feature). Gated like smoke.

[[bin]]

path = "src/cli/burst.rs"

spike = [
    "dep:tokio-tungstenite",

# The smoke client (T-09). Its TLS is the spike's (tokio-tungstenite over

# the lock.
smoke = [
    "dep:tokio-tungstenite",

burst = ["smoke"]

[dependencies]

axum = { version = "0.8", features = ["ws"] }

tokio = { version = "1", features = ["rt-multi-thread", "macros", "net", "sync", "time"] }

getrandom = "0.3"

subtle = "2.6"
# The room checks an affirmation's digest itself (ADR-0005 item 9), which is
# SHA-256. ring 0.17 was already in the lock and compiled in as rustls's

ring = { version = "0.17", default-features = false }

# --- spike-only, all optional -------------------------------------------------

# pulls, so client and server unify on one tungstenite in the lock.
tokio-tungstenite = { version = "0.29", optional = true, features = ["rustls-tls-webpki-roots"] }
# T-10: the room's own HTTPS client for Discord's three calls
# (`host/discord/http.rs`) rides rustls with ring, tokio-rustls and Mozilla's
# roots — all three were in the lock already (the spike and smoke used them as
# optional dependencies), so no crate was added. Default features off so there
# is no second crypto provider; `host/discord/http.rs` passes ring to its
# client explicitly. burst.rs and smoke.rs install ring as the process default
# themselves. native-tls would
# drag OpenSSL into any later Linux or CI run for no gain here.
rustls = { version = "0.23", default-features = false, features = ["ring"] }
tokio-rustls = { version = "0.26", default-features = false, features = ["ring"] }
webpki-roots = "1"
# SinkExt/StreamExt over the split socket. Already in the lock via axum. The
# room's transport uses it (T-04c), so it is no longer optional.
futures-util = { version = "0.3", default-features = false, features = ["sink", "std"] }
# serde and serde_json are no longer spike-only: the room itself uses them (T-04a),
# so they are ordinary dependencies above and the spike bins use those.

tokio-tungstenite = "0.29"

  1375	/// The files `mod name;` at `scope` in the file at `path` can load: `name.rs`
  1376	/// or `name/mod.rs`, inside each inline module around it. A `mod.rs`, and a
  1377	/// crate root that no `mod` loads as a module, load from their own directory.
  1378	/// Any other file loads from the directory named for it, or from its own when
  1379	/// a `#[path]` loaded it, so both are checked. `None` when an inline module
  1380	/// around it is itself conditional, which may carry a `#[path]` that moves it.
  1381	fn module_files(
  1382	    path: &Path,
  1383	    own_dir_only: bool,
  1384	    file: &FileImports,
  1385	    (scope, name): &(usize, String),
  1386	) -> Option<Vec<PathBuf>> {
  1387	    let mut inline = Vec::new();
  1388	    let mut at = Some(*scope);
  1389	    while let Some(scope) = at {
  1390	        match file.modules.get(&scope) {
  1391	            Some(declaration) => {
  1392	                if !file.declared.contains(declaration) {
  1393	                    return None;
  1394	                }
  1395	                inline.push(declaration.1.clone());
  1396	                at = Some(declaration.0);
  1397	            }
  1398	            None => at = file.parent[scope],
  1399	        }
  1400	    }
  1401	    let dir = path.parent().unwrap_or(Path::new(""));
  1402	    let mut bases = vec![dir.to_path_buf()];
  1403	    if !own_dir_only {
  1404	        bases.extend(path.file_stem().map(|stem| dir.join(stem)));
  1405	    }
  1406	    Some(
  1407	        bases
  1408	            .into_iter()
  1409	            .flat_map(|base| {
  1410	                let dir = inline
  1411	                    .iter()
  1412	                    .rev()
  1413	                    .fold(base, |dir, module| dir.join(module));
  1414	                [
  1415	                    dir.join(format!("{name}.rs")),
  1416	                    dir.join(name).join("mod.rs"),
  1417	                ]
  1418	            })
  1419	            .collect(),
  1420	    )
  1421	}

  1425	    rust_files(&crate_dir.join("src"), &mut paths);

**Specifying Rust module layout**
**Refining path-context handling**
codex
FAIL: against the pinned 1fbc6bd...1b42737 comparison, the original J/K/L/M repros are fixed, but four compiler/Cargo-accepted variants still bypass the source check, including three regressions from the new module-resolution logic. HEAD’s 10 dependency tests pass, M1–M3 are independently rejected, and the Rust checks passed 360 tests with 4 ignored; full `just test` completion was blocked by sandbox-denied uv-cache writes affecting the pipeline suite and one Python-backed web test, not by patch failures. The diff adds only the test file, with no production changes or folder-based denylist matching (`popquiz` denotes the Python package), and no worktree source or configuration files were edited during this review.

Full review comments:

- [P2] [Major] Bound macro-input imports to their token tree — /Users/michellerojas/rust-nyc-pop-quiz-worktrees/pq-47-room-dep-denylist/popquiz/tests/dependencies.rs:1155-1158
  Appending `macro_rules! m { (use std) => {} } #[cfg(any())] use openai::Client;` to `src/copy.rs` compiles, but all 10 dependency tests still pass. The reader treats the matcher’s `use std` as an import and searches beyond the macro’s closing delimiter for a semicolon; it consumes the real `use openai::Client;` without inspecting its root. J’s macro-input marking prevents false module declarations but does not prevent this remaining M3 bypass. Bound import consumption to its enclosing token tree while retaining detection of actual imports inside macro input.

- [P2] [Major] Respect disabled automatic binary discovery — /Users/michellerojas/rust-nyc-pop-quiz-worktrees/pq-47-room-dep-denylist/popquiz/tests/dependencies.rs:1302-1307
  The real manifest already sets `autobins = false`, but this function nevertheless classifies every `src/bin/*.rs` file as a crate root. A compiler-accepted repro is `mod bin;` in `src/lib.rs`, `mod helper;` in `src/bin/mod.rs`, `mod openai; #[cfg(any())] use openai::Client;` in `src/bin/helper.rs`, and `#![cfg(any())]` in `src/bin/helper/openai/mod.rs`. Rust loads the gated child under `helper/`, whereas the scanner incorrectly looks directly under `src/bin/` and exempts the import as local. HEAD passes all 10 tests on this copy; f82f7e0 rejects it. Only apply automatic-root classification when Cargo’s discovery settings enable it.

- [P2] [Major] Preserve the loading context of path-attributed modules — /Users/michellerojas/rust-nyc-pop-quiz-worktrees/pq-47-room-dep-denylist/popquiz/tests/dependencies.rs:1335-1338
  For `#[path = "support/helper.rs"] mod helper;` in `src/lib.rs`, Rust resolves a `mod openai;` inside that file to `src/support/openai/mod.rs`, not `src/support/helper/openai/mod.rs`. Put `mod openai; #[cfg(any())] use openai::Client;` in `helper.rs` and `#![cfg(any())]` in the actual child: this compiles, and HEAD passes all 10 dependency tests, while f82f7e0 rejects the import. The single-base change therefore regresses H for modules loaded through `#[path]`; marking the parent declaration conditional does not propagate its loading context into the separately scanned file. Track that context, or conservatively treat the child location as unresolved, rather than infer its base solely from the filename.

//! SPEC.md §11, the binding strings — mirrored from `web/shared/copy.js`.
//!
//! One constant per `copy.js` key, named as the key upper-cased, with the same
//! string. SPEC §11 is where copy is authored; `copy.js` is its port; this file
//! mirrors the port so the room's payloads carry exactly those strings.
//! `tests/twins.rs` walks the two against each other in both directions, so a

//! §11's Forbidden row and §11.1) run over `copy.js` in `web/test/copylint.test.js`,

//! Do not edit a string here. Edit SPEC §11, then `copy.js`, then this file.
//! Placeholders keep §11's ‹guillemet› form; [`fill`] substitutes them.

/// Substitute ‹placeholders›. Panics on a placeholder nobody supplied — the
/// same rule as `copy.js`'s `fill()`: a wall reading "‹n› of us said A" in front
/// of the room is worse than a loud failure at the call site.
pub fn fill(template: &str, values: &[(&str, &str)]) -> String {
    let mut out = String::with_capacity(template.len());
    let mut rest = template;
    while let Some(open) = rest.find('‹') {
        out.push_str(&rest[..open]);
        let after = &rest[open + '‹'.len_utf8()..];
        let close = after
            .find('›')
            .unwrap_or_else(|| panic!("copy::fill: unclosed placeholder in {template:?}"));
        let name = &after[..close];
        let value = values
            .iter()
            .find(|(k, _)| *k == name)
            .map(|(_, v)| *v)
            .unwrap_or_else(|| panic!("copy::fill: no value for ‹{name}› in {template:?}"));
        out.push_str(value);
        rest = &after[close + '›'.len_utf8()..];
    }
    out.push_str(rest);
    out
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    #[test]
    fn fill_substitutes_every_placeholder() {
        assert_eq!(
            fill(WALL_REVEAL_MOST_CHOSEN, &[("n", "24"), ("X", "A")]),
            "24 of us said A"
        );
        assert_eq!(fill(WALL_IDLE_TITLE, &[]), "Time for a pop quiz.");
    }

    #[test]
    #[should_panic(expected = "no value for ‹n›")]
    fn fill_refuses_a_missing_value() {
        fill(WALL_REVEAL_MOST_CHOSEN, &[("X", "A")]);
    }
}
